It deliberately avoids any in-process\n5\tSDK shortcut: every interaction with the child goes through the PTY's stdin /\n6\tstdout file descriptors, exactly as a human user would drive the REPL.\n7\t\n8\tThe class exposes the five method names enumerated in the COMP-007 roadmap row\n9\t(R-036) and the T02.16 acceptance criteria:\n10\t\n11\t* :meth:`PtyDriver.expect_prompt_ready` -- block until the child emits a\n12\t  \"prompt is ready for input\" indicator (``>`` or ``$`` on its own line by\n13\t  default; configurable via ``prompt_ready_pattern``).\n14\t* :meth:`PtyDriver.inject_prompt` -- write a user-style prompt to the child's\n15\t  stdin, terminated by a carriage return + line-feed, and flush.\n16\t* :meth:`PtyDriver.write_stdin` -- raw write to the child's stdin without any\n17\t  newline handling. Returns ``b\"\"`` immediately when no data is available.\n21\t* :meth:`PtyDriver.wait_exit` -- wait for the child to exit and return its\n22\t  exit code. Captures signal-termination as a negative exit code (POSIX\n23\t  convention) so callers can distinguish ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM``.\n24\t\n25\tThe vendored ptytest fork (T02.01) lives at\n26\t``src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`` and pins ``pexpect>=4.9`` via the vendored\n27\timports. This module first attempts to source ``pexpect`` from the vendored\n28\tlocation and falls back to the top-level ``pexpect`` package while the\n29\tvendored sources are still landing. Once T02.01 lands its sources, the\n30\tfall-back path becomes dead code -- this is intentional, not a long-term\n31\tescape hatch.\n32\t\n33\tTest stubs:\n34\t\n35\tThe unit tests in ``tests/cli/eval/test_pty_driver.py`` spawn a\n36\tself-contained Python stub (a small script that prints a prompt-ready\n37\tindicator, echoes a single line, and exits with a known code) rather than\n38\tthe real ``claude`` binary. This keeps the unit tests deterministic and\n39\thost-portable while still exercising the full PTY round-trip.\n40\t\"\"\"\n41\t\n42\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n43\t\n44\timport os\n45\timport re\n46\tfrom pathlib import Path\n47\tfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Pattern\n48\t\n49\tif TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover - typing only\n50\t    import pexpect as _pexpect_module\n51\t\n52\ttry:  # Prefer the vendored fork once T02.01 lands.\n53\t    from superclaude.cli.eval.pty import pexpect as _pexpect  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n54\texcept ImportError:  # pragma: no cover - exercised until T02.01 lands sources\n55\t    import pexpect as _pexpect\n56\t\n57\t__all__ = [\n58\t    \"DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN\",\n59\t    \"PtyDriver\",\n60\t    \"PtyDriverError\",\n61\t    \"PtyDriverTimeout\",\n62\t    \"PtyDriverNotStarted\",\n63\t    \"PtyDriverEOF\",\n64\t]\n65\t\n66\t\n67\t# Default prompt-ready pattern. Matches the two indicators Claude Code emits\n68\t# at the end of a \"ready for input\" frame: a bare ``>`` or ``$`` followed by\n69\t# optional whitespace and a CR/LF terminator. The pattern relies on an\n70\t# explicit newline rather than the ``$`` end-of-string anchor because\n71\t# ``pexpect`` compiles patterns without MULTILINE and the incremental PTY\n72\t# buffer almost always has trailing CR/LF after the marker -- an unanchored\n73\t# match against the newline is both more permissive and more reliable.\n74\tDEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"[>$] *\\r*\\n\"\n75\t\n76\t\n77\tclass PtyDriverError(Exception):\n78\t    \"\"\"Base class for ``PtyDriver`` failures.\"\"\"\n79\t\n80\t\n81\tclass PtyDriverTimeout(PtyDriverError):\n82\t    \"\"\"Raised when an ``expect`` or ``wait`` exceeded its timeout budget.\"\"\"\n83\t\n84\t\n85\tclass PtyDriverNotStarted(PtyDriverError):\n86\t    \"\"\"Raised when an interaction method is called before ``spawn`` completed.\"\"\"\n87\t\n88\t\n89\tclass PtyDriverEOF(PtyDriverError):\n90\t    \"\"\"Raised when the child closed its PTY before the expected event.\"\"\"\n91\t\n92\t\n93\tclass PtyDriver:\n94\t    \"\"\"Wraps ``pexpect.spawn`` for the IronClaude real-eval harness.\n95\t\n96\t    The constructor does **not** spawn the child; it only validates arguments\n97\t    and stores them. Call :meth:`spawn` (or use the class as a context manager)\n98\t    to actually launch the subprocess. This split keeps the constructor cheap\n99\t    and matches the cliEval orchestrator's \"build many drivers, spawn them in\n100\t    parallel\" pattern.\n101\t\n102\t    Parameters\n103\t    ----------\n104\t    command:\n105\t        Either a single executable string or a ``[command, *args]`` list.\n106\t        Mirrors the ``pexpect.spawn`` first-argument contract.\n107\t    env:\n108\t        Optional environment overlay. ``None`` inherits the parent process\n109\t        environment. To run with the per-eval HOME, pass\n110\t        ``HomeIsolation.env()`` here.\n111\t    cwd:\n112\t        Working directory for the child. ``None`` inherits the parent CWD.\n113\t    prompt_ready_pattern:\n114\t        Regex matched against the PTY output by\n115\t        :meth:`expect_prompt_ready`. The default\n126\t        ``(40, 120)`` matches Claude Code's recommended dev-host geometry.\n127\t    \"\"\"\n128\t\n129\t    def __init__(\n130\t        self,\n131\t        command: str | list[str],\n132\t        *,\n133\t        env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n134\t        cwd: Path | str | None = None,\n135\t        prompt_ready_pattern: str | Pattern[str] = DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN,\n136\t        default_timeout: float = 30.0,\n137\t        encoding: str | None = \"utf-8\",\n138\t        dimensions: tuple[int, int] = (40, 120),\n139\t    ) -> None:\n140\t        if isinstance(command, str):\n141\t            self._argv: list[str] = [command]\n142\t        else:\n143\t            if not command:\n144\t                raise ValueError(\"command must be a non-empty string or list\")\n145\t            self._argv = list(command)\n146\t\n147\t        self._env: dict[str, str] | None = dict(env) if env is not None else None\n148\t        self._cwd: str | None = str(cwd) if cwd is not None else None\n149\t\n150\t        if isinstance(prompt_ready_pattern, str):\n151\t            self._prompt_ready_pattern: Pattern[str] = re.compile(prompt_ready_pattern)\n152\t        else:\n153\t            self._prompt_ready_pattern = prompt_ready_pattern\n154\t\n155\t        if default_timeout <= 0:\n156\t            raise ValueError(\"default_timeout must be > 0\")\n157\t        self._default_timeout = float(default_timeout)\n158\t        self._encoding = encoding\n159\t        self._dimensions = dimensions\n160\t\n161\t        self._child: Any | None = None  # Pass an explicit value to override on a\n231\t            per-call basis.\n232\t\n233\t        Returns\n234\t        -------\n235\t        str\n236\t            The bytes captured *before* the prompt-ready match. Useful when\n237\t            the caller wants to inspect banner / version output the child\n238\t            emitted prior to becoming interactive.\n239\t\n240\t        Raises\n241\t        ------\n242\t        PtyDriverTimeout\n243\t            The prompt-ready pattern did not appear within ``timeout``\n244\t            seconds.\n245\t        PtyDriverEOF\n246\t            The child closed its PTY (typically a crash) before emitting\n247\t            the prompt-ready pattern.\n248\t        \"\"\"\n249\t\n250\t        child = self._require_child()\n251\t        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n252\t        try:\n253\t            child.expect(self._prompt_ready_pattern, timeout=effective_timeout)\n254\t        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n255\t            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n256\t                f\"prompt_ready_pattern not seen within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n257\t            ) from exc\n258\t        except _pexpect.EOF as exc:\n259\t            raise PtyDriverEOF(\n260\t                \"child closed PTY before prompt-ready pattern matched\"\n261\t            ) from exc\n262\t        before = child.before\n263\t        return before if isinstance(before, str) else (before or b\"\").decode(\n264\t            self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\"\n265\t        )\n266\t\n267\t    def inject_prompt(self, text: str) -> None:\n268\t        \"\"\"Send a user-style prompt to the child, terminated by ``\\\\r\\\\n``.\n269\t\n270\t        Wraps ``pexpect.spawn.send`` with the carriage-return + line-feed\n271\t        sequence Claude Code expects from a real TTY. The send is flushed\n272\t        before returning.\n273\t        \"\"\"\n274\t\n275\t        child = self._require_child()\n276\t        if not isinstance(text, str):\n277\t            raise TypeError(\"inject_prompt requires str (use write_stdin for bytes)\")\n278\t        child.send(text + \"\\r\\n\")\n279\t        # pexpect.spawn writes via os.write which is unbuffered, but be explicit\n280\t        # in case a subclass or vendored fork swaps the I/O layer.\n281\t        flush = getattr(child, \"flush\", None)\n282\t        if callable(flush):\n283\t            try:\n284\t                flush()\n285\t            except (OSError, ValueError):\n286\t                pass\n287\t\n288\t    def write_stdin(self, data: str | bytes) -> int:\n289\t        \"\"\"Raw write to the child's stdin.\n290\t\n291\t        Returns the number of bytes (or characters when ``encoding`` is set)\n292\t        that pexpect reports it wrote. No newline is appended; callers that\n293\t        want a prompt-style write should use :meth:`inject_prompt`.\n294\t        \"\"\"\n295\t\n296\t        child = self._require_child()\n297\t        if isinstance(data, bytes) and self._encoding is not None:\n298\t            data = data.decode(self._encoding, errors=\"strict\")\n299\t        result = child.send(data)\n300\t        return int(result) if result is not None else len(data)\n301\t\n302\t    def read_stdout(self, size: int = -1, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n303\t        \"\"\"Non-blocking read of up to ``size`` bytes from the child PTY.\n304\t\n305\t        ``size`` is the maximum chunk size; negative values map to \"as much\n306\t        as the PTY currently holds, capped at 65536 bytes.\" Returns an empty\n307\t        string when no data is currently buffered (instead of blocking).\n308\t\n309\t        ``timeout`` is the maximum time pexpect will wait for the first byte.\n310\t        ``None`` uses the driver's ``default_timeout``; pass ``0`` for a true\n311\t        non-blocking poll.\n312\t        \"\"\"\n313\t\n314\t        child = self._require_child()\n315\t        cap = 65536 if size is None or size < 0 else int(size)\n316\t        if cap <= 0:\n317\t            return \"\"\n318\t        wait = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n319\t        try:\n320\t            chunk = child.read_nonblocking(size=cap, timeout=wait)\n321\t        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT:\n322\t            return \"\"\n323\t        except _pexpect.EOF:\n324\t            return \"\"\n325\t        if isinstance(chunk, bytes):\n326\t            chunk = chunk.decode(self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n327\t        return chunk\n328\t\n329\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ exit\n330\t\n331\t    def wait_exit(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> int:\n332\t        \"\"\"Wait for the child to exit and return its exit code.\n333\t\n334\t        Parameters\n335\t        ----------\n336\t        timeout:\n337\t            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's default timeout.\n338\t\n339\t        Returns\n340\t        -------\n341\t        int\n342\t            POSIX exit code. Signal-terminated children return a negative\n343\t            value (``-SIGTERM`` = ``-15``, etc.) so callers can distinguish\n344\t            ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM`` cleanly.\n345\t\n346\t        Raises\n347\t        ------\n348\t        PtyDriverTimeout\n349\t            The child was still alive after ``timeout`` seconds. The child\n350\t            remains running; call :meth:`terminate` to clean it up.\n351\t        \"\"\"\n352\t\n353\t        child = self._require_child()\n354\t        if self._exit_code is not None and not child.isalive():\n355\t            return self._exit_code\n356\t\n357\t        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n358\t        # pexpect.wait() doesn't accept a timeout; emulate one by polling\n359\t        # ``expect(EOF, timeout=...)`` which blocks until the PTY closes.\n360\t        try:\n361\t            child.expect(_pexpect.EOF, timeout=effective_timeout)\n362\t        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n363\t            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n364\t                f\"child did not exit within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n365\t            ) from exc\n366\t\n367\t        # Wait reaps the zombie and populates exitstatus / signalstatus.\n368\t        try:\n369\t            child.wait()\n370\t        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n371\t            # ``wait()`` raises if pexpect believes the child is still alive;\n372\t            # in our flow EOF already arrived, so any residual race is\n373\t            # benign -- isalive() will return False and exitstatus / signalstatus\n374\t            # will be set on the next attribute read.\n375\t            pass\n376\t\n377\t        if child.signalstatus is not None:\n378\t            self._exit_code = -int(child.signalstatus)\n379\t        else:\n380\t            self._exit_code = int(child.exitstatus) if child.exitstatus is not None else -1\n381\t        return self._exit_code\n382\t\n383\t    # It deliberately avoids any in-process\nSDK shortcut: every interaction with the child goes through the PTY's stdin /\nstdout file descriptors, exactly as a human user would drive the REPL.\n\nThe class exposes the five method names enumerated in the COMP-007 roadmap row\n(R-036) and the T02.16 acceptance criteria:\n\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.expect_prompt_ready` -- block until the child emits a\n  \"prompt is ready for input\" indicator (``>`` or ``$`` on its own line by\n  default; configurable via ``prompt_ready_pattern``).\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.inject_prompt` -- write a user-style prompt to the child's\n  stdin, terminated by a carriage return + line-feed, and flush.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.write_stdin` -- raw write to the child's stdin without any\n  newline handling. Used for control sequences, partial-line input, or\n  test-stub probing.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.read_stdout` -- non-blocking read of the next chunk of bytes\n  from the PTY. Returns ``b\"\"`` immediately when no data is available.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.wait_exit` -- wait for the child to exit and return its\n  exit code. Captures signal-termination as a negative exit code (POSIX\n  convention) so callers can distinguish ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM``.\n\nThe vendored ptytest fork (T02.01) lives at\n``src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`` and pins ``pexpect>=4.9`` via the vendored\nimports. This module first attempts to source ``pexpect`` from the vendored\nlocation and falls back to the top-level ``pexpect`` package while the\nvendored sources are still landing. Once T02.01 lands its sources, the\nfall-back path becomes dead code -- this is intentional, not a long-term\nescape hatch.\n\nTest stubs:\n\nThe unit tests in ``tests/cli/eval/test_pty_driver.py`` spawn a\nself-contained Python stub (a small script that prints a prompt-ready\nindicator, echoes a single line, and exits with a known code) rather than\nthe real ``claude`` binary. Matches the two indicators Claude Code emits\n# at the end of a \"ready for input\" frame: a bare ``>`` or ``$`` followed by\n# optional whitespace and a CR/LF terminator. The pattern relies on an\n# explicit newline rather than the ``$`` end-of-string anchor because\n# ``pexpect`` compiles patterns without MULTILINE and the incremental PTY\n# buffer almost always has trailing CR/LF after the marker -- an unanchored\n# match against the newline is both more permissive and more reliable.\nDEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"[>$] *\\r*\\n\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Base class for ``PtyDriver`` failures.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverTimeout(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an ``expect`` or ``wait`` exceeded its timeout budget.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverNotStarted(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an interaction method is called before ``spawn`` completed.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverEOF(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when the child closed its PTY before the expected event.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriver:\n    \"\"\"Wraps ``pexpect.spawn`` for the IronClaude real-eval harness.\n\n    The constructor does **not** spawn the child; it only validates arguments\n    and stores them. Call :meth:`spawn` (or use the class as a context manager)\n    to actually launch the subprocess. This split keeps the constructor cheap\n    and matches the cliEval orchestrator's \"build many drivers, spawn them in\n    parallel\" pattern.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    command:\n        Either a single executable string or a ``[command, *args]`` list.\n        Mirrors the ``pexpect.spawn`` first-argument contract.\n    env:\n        Optional environment overlay. ``None`` inherits the parent process\n        environment. The default\n        ``(40, 120)`` matches Claude Code's recommended dev-host geometry.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        command: str | list[str],\n        *,\n        env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n        cwd: Path | str | None = None,\n        prompt_ready_pattern: str | Pattern[str] = DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN,\n        default_timeout: float = 30.0,\n        encoding: str | None = \"utf-8\",\n        dimensions: tuple[int, int] = (40, 120),\n    ) -> None:\n        if isinstance(command, str):\n            self._argv: list[str] = [command]\n        else:\n            if not command:\n                raise ValueError(\"command must be a non-empty string or list\")\n            self._argv = list(command)\n\n        self._env: dict[str, str] | None = dict(env) if env is not None else None\n        self._cwd: str | None = str(cwd) if cwd is not None else None\n\n        if isinstance(prompt_ready_pattern, str):\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern: Pattern[str] = re.compile(prompt_ready_pattern)\n        else:\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern = prompt_ready_pattern\n\n        if default_timeout <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"default_timeout must be > 0\")\n        self._default_timeout = float(default_timeout)\n        self._encoding = encoding\n        self._dimensions = dimensions\n\n        self._child: Any | None = None  # pexpect.spawn instance once spawned\n        self._exit_code: int | None = None\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ lifecycle\n\n    def spawn(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Launch the child subprocess via ``pexpect.spawn``.\n\n        Idempotent: a second call after the child has exited starts a fresh\n        process; a second call while the child is still alive raises\n        :class:`PtyDriverError`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is not None and self.is_alive():\n            raise PtyDriverError(\"PtyDriver.spawn called while child is still alive\")\n\n        rows, cols = self._dimensions\n        argv0, *args = self._argv\n        self._child = _pexpect.spawn(\n            argv0,\n            args=args,\n            env=self._env,\n            cwd=self._cwd,\n            timeout=self._default_timeout,\n            encoding=self._encoding,\n            dimensions=(rows, cols),\n        )\n        self._exit_code = None\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"PtyDriver\":\n        self.spawn()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n        try:\n            if self.is_alive():\n                self.terminate()\n        finally:\n            self.close()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ state\n\n    def is_alive(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` while the child is running.\"\"\"\n\n        return self._child is not None and bool(self._child.isalive())\n\n    @property\n    def pid(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"PID of the child process, or ``None`` if not yet spawned.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return None\n        return int(self._child.pid)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ I/O\n\n    def _require_child(self) -> Any:\n        if self._child is None:\n            raise PtyDriverNotStarted(\"spawn() must be called before interaction\")\n        return self._child\n\n    def expect_prompt_ready(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Block until the child emits the prompt-ready indicator.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's\n            ``default_timeout``. Pass an explicit value to override on a\n            per-call basis.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        str\n            The bytes captured *before* the prompt-ready match. Useful when\n            the caller wants to inspect banner / version output the child\n            emitted prior to becoming interactive.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The prompt-ready pattern did not appear within ``timeout``\n            seconds.\n        PtyDriverEOF\n            The child closed its PTY (typically a crash) before emitting\n            the prompt-ready pattern.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            child.expect(self._prompt_ready_pattern, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"prompt_ready_pattern not seen within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n        except _pexpect.EOF as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverEOF(\n                \"child closed PTY before prompt-ready pattern matched\"\n            ) from exc\n        before = child.before\n        return before if isinstance(before, str) else (before or b\"\").decode(\n            self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\"\n        )\n\n    def inject_prompt(self, text: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send a user-style prompt to the child, terminated by ``\\\\r\\\\n``.\n\n        Wraps ``pexpect.spawn.send`` with the carriage-return + line-feed\n        sequence Claude Code expects from a real TTY. The send is flushed\n        before returning.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if not isinstance(text, str):\n            raise TypeError(\"inject_prompt requires str (use write_stdin for bytes)\")\n        child.send(text + \"\\r\\n\")\n        # pexpect.spawn writes via os.write which is unbuffered, but be explicit\n        # in case a subclass or vendored fork swaps the I/O layer.\n        flush = getattr(child, \"flush\", None)\n        if callable(flush):\n            try:\n                flush()\n            except (OSError, ValueError):\n                pass\n\n    def write_stdin(self, data: str | bytes) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Raw write to the child's stdin.\n\n        Returns the number of bytes (or characters when ``encoding`` is set)\n        that pexpect reports it wrote. No newline is appended; callers that\n        want a prompt-style write should use :meth:`inject_prompt`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if isinstance(data, bytes) and self._encoding is not None:\n            data = data.decode(self._encoding, errors=\"strict\")\n        result = child.send(data)\n        return int(result) if result is not None else len(data)\n\n    def read_stdout(self, size: int = -1, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Non-blocking read of up to ``size`` bytes from the child PTY.\n\n        ``size`` is the maximum chunk size; negative values map to \"as much\n        as the PTY currently holds, capped at 65536 bytes.\" Returns an empty\n        string when no data is currently buffered (instead of blocking).\n\n        ``timeout`` is the maximum time pexpect will wait for the first byte.\n        ``None`` uses the driver's ``default_timeout``; pass ``0`` for a true\n        non-blocking poll.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        cap = 65536 if size is None or size < 0 else int(size)\n        if cap <= 0:\n            return \"\"\n        wait = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            chunk = child.read_nonblocking(size=cap, timeout=wait)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT:\n            return \"\"\n        except _pexpect.EOF:\n            return \"\"\n        if isinstance(chunk, bytes):\n            chunk = chunk.decode(self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n        return chunk\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ exit\n\n    def wait_exit(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Wait for the child to exit and return its exit code.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's default timeout.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        int\n            POSIX exit code. Signal-terminated children return a negative\n            value (``-SIGTERM`` = ``-15``, etc.) so callers can distinguish\n            ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM`` cleanly.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The child was still alive after ``timeout`` seconds. The child\n            remains running; call :meth:`terminate` to clean it up.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if self._exit_code is not None and not child.isalive():\n            return self._exit_code\n\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        # pexpect.wait() doesn't accept a timeout; force-close to\n            # avoid leaking the PTY fd. terminate() is safe to call twice.\n            try:\n                self._child.close(force=True)\n            except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n                pass\n        if self._child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(self._child.signalstatus)\n        elif self._child.exitstatus is not None and self._exit_code is None:\n            self._exit_code = int(self._child.exitstatus)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers\n\n    @property\n    def exit_code(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"Last-captured exit code (``None`` until the child has exited).\"\"\"\n\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    def fileno(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Return the PTY's read file descriptor for ``select``-based loops.\"\"\"\n\n        return int(self._require_child().fileno())\n","numLines":426,"startLine":1,"totalLines":426}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019mkpC4LEw8vExhPiMrJAJB","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"COMP-013 ClaudeProcess reuse adapter (Task T02.19 / D-0038).\n2\t\n3\tFR-G1 requires every eval to drive the real ``claude`` binary as a child\n4\tprocess; the cliEval harness must never short-circuit through an\n5\tin-process ``anthropic`` SDK call. This module is the single integration\n6\tpoint between :class:`~superclaude.cli.eval.isolation.HomeIsolation` (per-\n7\teval HOME + ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` env layer) and\n8\t:class:`~superclaude.cli.pipeline.process.ClaudeProcess` (the production\n9\tsubprocess lifecycle manager with signal handling, ``--print``-mode\n10\tcommand building, stdout/stderr file separation, and timeout-aware\n11\t``wait`` / ``terminate``).\n12\t\n13\tThe adapter has three jobs:\n14\t\n15\t1. **Reuse, do not duplicate.** Delegate command construction\n16\t   (``build_command``) and base env construction (``build_env``) to\n17\t   :class:`ClaudeProcess`. The adapter inherits this\n40\tseparation verbatim — the AC requires distinct stdout / stderr\n41\tartifacts for downstream gate scoring, and routing the spawn through\n42\t``ClaudeProcess`` (rather than re-implementing the popen pattern) is\n43\tthe simplest way to keep that guarantee.\n44\t\n45\tPtyDriver versus ClaudeProcess\n46\t==============================\n47\t\n48\tThe cliEval harness has two real-claude entry points:\n49\t\n50\t* :class:`~superclaude.cli.eval.pty_driver.PtyDriver` (T02.16,\n51\t  COMP-007) drives the *interactive* ``claude`` REPL through a\n52\t  pseudo-terminal. Used by the Expect-style assertion layer\n53\t  (T04.07).\n54\t* :class:`ClaudeProcessAdapter` (this module, COMP-013) drives\n55\t  the *non-interactive* ``claude --print`` mode through\n56\t  :class:`ClaudeProcess`. Used by capability gate tests and\n57\t  any eval that wants a single-shot transcript with separated\n58\t  stdout / stderr.\n59\t\n60\tBoth satisfy FR-G1 (real subprocess discipline). They are not\n61\tinterchangeable: the PTY path merges stdout and stderr into one stream\n62\t(unavoidable in a TTY), while ``ClaudeProcess`` keeps them split. The\n63\tadapter therefore lives beside ``PtyDriver`` instead of replacing it.\n64\t\n65\tCross-links\n66\t===========\n67\t\n68\t* COMP-006 :class:`HomeIsolation` (T02.11 / D-0032) — supplies the\n69\t  per-eval env via :meth:`HomeIsolation.env`.\n70\t* COMP-007 :class:`PtyDriver` (T02.16 / D-0036) — sibling real-claude\n71\t  spawner for interactive mode.\n72\t* :class:`~superclaude.cli.pipeline.process.ClaudeProcess` (lines\n73\t  24-150 of ``cli/pipeline/process.py``) — wrapped here.\n74\t* Ruff config: ``[tool.ruff.lint.flake8-tidy-imports.banned-api]`` in\n75\t  ``pyproject.toml`` — registers the ``anthropic`` ban.\n76\t\"\"\"\n77\t\n78\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n79\t\n80\timport os\n81\tfrom pathlib import Path\n82\tfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Callable\n83\t\n84\tfrom superclaude.cli.pipeline.process import ClaudeProcess\n85\t\n86\tif TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover - typing only\n87\t    from superclaude.cli.eval.isolation import HomeIsolation\n88\t\n89\t__all__ = [\n90\t    \"ClaudeProcessAdapter\",\n91\t    \"ClaudeProcessAdapterError\",\n92\t]\n93\t\n94\t\n95\tclass ClaudeProcessAdapterError(Exception):\n96\t    \"\"\"Raised by :class:`ClaudeProcessAdapter` on configuration failures.\n97\t\n98\t    The adapter itself never wraps :class:`ClaudeProcess` runtime\n99\t    exceptions — those propagate verbatim so the caller's existing\n100\t    ``ClaudeProcess`` error handling (timeout 124, signal handling)\n101\t    keeps working. This exception type covers only adapter-local\n102\t    misuse: a ``cwd`` argument that does not exist on disk, a\n103\t    :class:`HomeIsolation` whose ``setup`` has not run yet, etc.\n104\t    \"\"\"\n105\t\n106\t\n107\tclass ClaudeProcessAdapter:\n108\t    \"\"\"Wrap :class:`ClaudeProcess` for per-eval HOME-isolated spawning.\n109\t\n110\t    The adapter exists to:\n111\t\n112\t    * Inject :meth:`HomeIsolation.env` into the subprocess env (HOME,\n113\t      CLAUDE_SESSION_ID, optional CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET).\n114\t    * Pin the child's working directory by ``chdir``-ing the parent\n115\t      around :meth:`ClaudeProcess.start` (Popen inherits cwd at fork).\n116\t    * Re-export ``ClaudeProcess``'s stdout / stderr file separation\n117\t      without changes.\n118\t\n119\t    The class deliberately mirrors :class:`ClaudeProcess`'s constructor\n120\t    parameters so callers familiar with the upstream lifecycle do not\n121\t    need to relearn the surface; the only structural addition is the\n122\t    ``home`` and ``cwd`` keyword arguments. This module is the single integration\npoint between :class:`~superclaude.cli.eval.isolation.HomeIsolation` (per-\neval HOME + ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` env layer) and\n:class:`~superclaude.cli.pipeline.process.ClaudeProcess` (the production\nsubprocess lifecycle manager with signal handling, ``--print``-mode\ncommand building, stdout/stderr file separation, and timeout-aware\n``wait`` / ``terminate``).\n\nThe adapter has three jobs:\n\n1. **Reuse, do not duplicate.** Delegate command construction\n   (``build_command``) and base env construction (``build_env``) to\n   :class:`ClaudeProcess`. The adapter assembles the per-eval env by\n   merging :meth:`HomeIsolation.env` *on top of* whatever extra env vars\n   the caller supplied so ``HOME`` / ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` always win.\n2. **Pin cwd.** ``ClaudeProcess`` itself does not expose a ``cwd``\n   Popen kwarg today; the adapter holds the cwd argument and ``chdir``-s\n   into it around :meth:`ClaudeProcess.start` so the forked child\n   inherits the desired working directory. The chdir is fenced by a\n   try/finally so the parent's cwd is always restored, even on a spawn\n   exception.\n3. **Reinforce FR-G1.** The companion ruff lint rule (registered in\n   ``pyproject.toml`` under ``[tool.ruff.lint.flake8-tidy-imports.\n   banned-api]``) rejects any ``anthropic`` SDK import. ``ruff check\n   src/superclaude/cli/eval/`` therefore fires on a synthetic\n   ``import anthropic`` anywhere in this subtree before the adapter\n   even runs. The two layers (subprocess discipline + lint rule)\n   together close the FR-G1 surface.\n\nStdout/stderr separation\n========================\n\n:class:`ClaudeProcess` opens ``output_file`` and ``error_file`` as\n*separate* real file handles and passes them as ``stdout=`` and\n``stderr=`` to :func:`subprocess.Popen`. The adapter inherits this\nseparation verbatim — the AC requires distinct stdout / stderr\nartifacts for downstream gate scoring, and routing the spawn through\n``ClaudeProcess`` (rather than re-implementing the popen pattern) is\nthe simplest way to keep that guarantee.\n\nPtyDriver versus ClaudeProcess\n==============================\n\nThe cliEval harness has two real-claude entry points:\n\n* :class:`~superclaude.cli.eval.pty_driver.PtyDriver` (T02.16,\n  COMP-007) drives the *interactive* ``claude`` REPL through a\n  pseudo-terminal. Used by capability gate tests and\n  any eval that wants a single-shot transcript with separated\n  stdout / stderr.\n\nBoth satisfy FR-G1 (real subprocess discipline). They are not\ninterchangeable: the PTY path merges stdout and stderr into one stream\n(unavoidable in a TTY), while ``ClaudeProcess`` keeps them split. The\nadapter therefore lives beside ``PtyDriver`` instead of replacing it.\n\nCross-links\n===========\n\n* COMP-006 :class:`HomeIsolation` (T02.11 / D-0032) — supplies the\n  per-eval env via :meth:`HomeIsolation.env`.\n* COMP-007 :class:`PtyDriver` (T02.16 / D-0036) — sibling real-claude\n  spawner for interactive mode.\n* :class:`~superclaude.cli.pipeline.process.ClaudeProcess` (lines\n  24-150 of ``cli/pipeline/process.py``) — wrapped here.\n* Ruff config: ``[tool.ruff.lint.flake8-tidy-imports.banned-api]`` in\n  ``pyproject.toml`` — registers the ``anthropic`` ban.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Callable\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.pipeline.process import ClaudeProcess\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover - typing only\n    from superclaude.cli.eval.isolation import HomeIsolation\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"ClaudeProcessAdapter\",\n    \"ClaudeProcessAdapterError\",\n]\n\n\nclass ClaudeProcessAdapterError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised by :class:`ClaudeProcessAdapter` on configuration failures.\n\n    The adapter itself never wraps :class:`ClaudeProcess` runtime\n    exceptions — those propagate verbatim so the caller's existing\n    ``ClaudeProcess`` error handling (timeout 124, signal handling)\n    keeps working. Build a fresh :class:`ClaudeProcess` instance (so the\n           adapter can be re-spawned without leaking the prior child).\n        2. ``chdir`` the parent process to :attr:`cwd`. A separate copy\n   pipeline would drift; the COMP-014 reuse contract pins this to the\n   one installer.\n2. **Deploy ``hooks.json`` verbatim.** ``install_hooks`` merges hook\n   registrations *into* ``settings.json`` but never copies the source\n   ``hooks.json`` to the target HOME. The acceptance criteria for T02.14\n   require ``<home_path>/.claude/hooks.json`` to be byte-identical to\n   ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`` so post-mortem inspection and the\n   FR-G* gate tests can SHA256-compare the source-of-truth registrations\n   against what the per-eval HOME shipped. The adapter performs this\n   second copy itself.\n\nDefense-in-depth: a pre-flight refusal asserts ``home_path`` is not the\nreal ``~/.claude/`` (resolves both sides; rejects any equality or\ndescendant relationship). This complements but does not replace the\n:class:`HomeIsolation` containment guard — the adapter MAY be invoked\ndirectly by tests, so the guard must live here too.\n\nIdempotency: re-invoking ``deploy_hooks_to`` against the same\n``home_path`` produces identical filesystem state. ``install_hooks``\nitself is idempotent (existing scripts are skipped without ``--force``;\nthe settings.json merge skips matcher collisions). The verbatim\n``hooks.json`` copy uses ``shutil.copy2`` which overwrites with the same\nbytes when the source is unchanged.\n\nError contract: any failure inside the adapter raises\n:class:`HookDeployFailed` with an :attr:`HookDeployFailed.error_tag`\nattribute the EvalRunner (T03.x) propagates into\n``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts``. The tag is a short kebab-case\nidentifier the orchestrator buckets ERRORED evals by without parsing\nhuman-readable strings.\n\nCross-links\n===========\n\n* COMP-006 :class:`HomeIsolation` (T02.11 / D-0032) — supplies the\n  ``home_path`` argument.\n* NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13 / D-0033) — invokes the\n  adapter from inside ``HomeIsolation.setup``'s post-mkdtemp block in\n  a future wiring task.\n* :func:`superclaude.cli.install_hooks.install_hooks` — the underlying\n  installer this adapter wraps.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport shutil\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom ..install_hooks import install_hooks\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"HookDeployFailed\",\n    \"deploy_hooks_to\",\n]\n\n\n# Relative paths under the per-eval HOME that the adapter writes to.\n_SETTINGS_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"settings.json\")\n_HOOKS_JSON_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"hooks.json\")\n\n\nclass HookDeployFailed(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised by :func:`deploy_hooks_to` when hook deployment cannot complete.\n\n    Carries a short kebab-case :attr:`error_tag` the orchestrator\n    (:class:`EvalRunner`, T03.x) routes into\n    ``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts`` so failed evals can be bucketed\n    without parsing the human-readable message.\n\n    Attributes:\n        error_tag: Short identifier for the failure mode. The\n              installer's message is preserved in :attr:`detail`.\n            * ``\"hooks-json-copy-failed\"`` — the verbatim\n              ``hooks.json`` copy raised :class:`OSError`. The OS\n              error message is preserved in :attr:`detail`.\n            * ``\"source-hooks-json-missing\"`` — the source\n              ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`` is not on disk.\n              Should never happen in a working checkout; surfaced\n              explicitly to make missing-source bugs loud.\n\n        home_path: The per-eval HOME the adapter was asked to deploy to.\n        detail: Human-readable failure detail (installer message,\n            ``OSError`` rendering, etc.). Never contains the error_tag\n            itself so the two surfaces stay independent.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        error_tag: str,\n        home_path: Path,\n        detail: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        self.error_tag = error_tag\n        self.home_path = home_path\n        self.detail = detail\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"COMP-014 hook deploy failed [{error_tag}] for home_path={home_path!s}: {detail}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef _real_user_claude_dir() -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve the user's real ``~/.claude/`` directory.\n\n    Returns the resolved (symlinks collapsed) path. Used by the\n    defense-in-depth refusal so a per-eval ``home_path`` that is a\n    symlink to the real user HOME still fails the equality / descendant\n    check.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return (Path.home() / \".claude\").resolve(strict=False)\n\n\ndef _refuse_real_home(home_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Reject ``home_path`` when any adapter write would land under real ``~/.claude/``.\n\n    Two attack surfaces are caught:\n\n    * ``home_path`` itself resolves to (or under) the real\n      ``~/.claude/`` directory — every adapter write is anchored at\n      ``home_path``, so this catches direct attempts.\n    * ``home_path / .claude`` resolves to (or under) the real\n      ``~/.claude/`` — the subtle case where the caller passes their\n      own ``$HOME`` as ``home_path`` and the adapter's\n      ``<home_path>/.claude/settings.json`` write would otherwise\n      silently target the real configuration directory.\n\n    Both sides are resolved with ``strict=False`` so a symlink in either\n    direction is collapsed before comparison.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    real = _real_user_claude_dir()\n    resolved_home = home_path.resolve(strict=False)\n    resolved_claude_subdir = (home_path / \".claude\").resolve(strict=False)\n\n    def _matches(candidate: Path) -> bool:\n        return candidate == real or candidate.is_relative_to(real)\n\n    if _matches(resolved_home) or _matches(resolved_claude_subdir):\n        raise HookDeployFailed(\n            error_tag=\"refused-real-home\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            detail=(\n                f\"home_path {home_path!s} (resolved={resolved_home!s}; Imported via the public ``install_hooks`` to avoid coupling\n    on a private helper that may move.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # ``hook_adapter.py`` lives at src/superclaude/cli/eval/hook_adapter.py.\n    # Two parents up is the package root (src/superclaude/).\n    package_root = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent\n    return package_root / \"hooks\" / \"hooks.json\"\n\n\ndef deploy_hooks_to(home_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Deploy SuperClaude hooks into the per-eval HOME at ``home_path``.\n\n    Two filesystem effects, in this order:\n\n    1. :func:`install_hooks` is invoked with ``target_path`` resolved to\n       ``<home_path>/.claude/settings.json``. This copies the hook\n       scripts to ``<home_path>/.claude/hooks/`` and merges hook\n       registrations into a fresh ``<home_path>/.claude/settings.json``.\n    2. The installer skips already-deployed\n    scripts and matcher-colliding registrations; the verbatim copy\n    overwrites with the same bytes when the source is unchanged.\n\n    Args:\n        home_path: Per-eval HOME directory created by\n            :class:`HomeIsolation.setup`. MUST NOT resolve to the real\n            ``~/.claude/`` directory — the pre-flight guard rejects\n            that case before any write happens.\n\n    Raises:\n        HookDeployFailed: Any of the failure modes documented on\n            :class:`HookDeployFailed.error_tag`. Each failure mode\n            carries enough detail (``home_path`` + ``detail``) for the\n            orchestrator to bucket the eval and surface the underlying\n            cause in operator-facing reports.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Step 0 — defense-in-depth refusal. Must run BEFORE any write so\n    # `install_hooks` never even materializes a directory under real\n    # `~/.claude/`.\n    _refuse_real_home(home_path)\n\n    source_hooks_json = _source_hooks_json()\n    if not source_hooks_json.exists():\n        raise HookDeployFailed(\n            error_tag=\"source-hooks-json-missing\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            detail=f\"source hooks.json not found at {source_hooks_json!s}\",\n        )\n\n    # Step 1 — delegate scripts + settings.json merge to install_hooks.\n    settings_path = home_path.joinpath(*_SETTINGS_RELPATH)\n    success, message = install_hooks(target_path=settings_path, force=False)\n    if not success:\n        raise HookDeployFailed(\n            error_tag=\"install-hooks-failed\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            detail=message,\n        )\n\n    # Step 2 — verbatim hooks.json copy. Required by the AC: the\n    # per-eval HOME must carry a SHA256-identical copy of the source\n    # registrations so post-mortem inspection / FR-G* gate tests can\n    # diff against the canonical file. Routed through the private\n    # :func:`_copy_hooks_json_verbatim` helper so test patches against\n    # ``shutil.copy2`` (a globally shared module attribute) cannot\n    # accidentally also break ``install_hooks``'s own copy pipeline.\n    dest_hooks_json = home_path.joinpath(*_HOOKS_JSON_RELPATH)\n    try:\n        dest_hooks_json.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        _copy_hooks_json_verbatim(source_hooks_json, dest_hooks_json)\n    except OSError as exc:\n        raise HookDeployFailed(\n            error_tag=\"hooks-json-copy-failed\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            detail=f\"verbatim hooks.json copy from {source_hooks_json!s} \"\n            f\"to {dest_hooks_json!s} failed: {exc}\",\n        ) from exc\n\n\ndef _copy_hooks_json_verbatim(src: Path, dest: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Copy ``src`` to ``dest`` verbatim (byte-identical, preserving mtime).\n\n    Wraps :func:`shutil.copy2` so the adapter's verbatim copy site is\n    a dedicated, monkeypatch-friendly seam. Tests targeting the\n    ``hooks-json-copy-failed`` error tag patch this function instead\n    of the shared ``shutil.copy2`` global, which would also break\n    :func:`install_hooks` (it copies hook scripts through the same\n    ``shutil.copy2`` API).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    shutil.copy2(src, dest)\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":56,"oldLines":7,"newStart":56,"newLines":7,"lines":[" import shutil"," from pathlib import Path"," ","-from ..install_hooks import install_hooks","+from superclaude.cli.install_hooks import install_hooks"," "," __all__ = [","     \"HookDeployFailed\","]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

Field shape matches the JSONL contract documented\n    in ``artifacts/D-0049/spec.md``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    event: str\n    ts_offset_sec: float\n    eval_id: str\n    step: str\n    extra: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        # Build an ordered dict so JSON diff output stays stable; the\n        # round() pin is documented in D-0049/spec.md (\"Rounded to\n        # microseconds so JSON diff output stays compact and stable.\").\n        return {\n            \"event\": self.event,\n            \"ts_offset_sec\": round(self.ts_offset_sec, 6),\n            \"eval_id\": self.eval_id,\n            \"step\": self.step,\n            \"extra\": dict(self.extra),\n        }\n\n\nclass _JsonlLog:\n    \"\"\"Thread-safe buffer + writer for the per-eval JSONL log.\n\n    The runner emits events from both the worker thread (every\n    lifecycle step except timeout teardown) and the main thread (the\n    ``timeout_fired`` + ``outcome`` events). A lock keeps the ordered\n    list of buffered events consistent and `write()` deterministic.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, *, eval_id: str, clock: Callable[[], float]) -> None:\n        self._eval_id = eval_id\n        self._clock = clock\n        self._start = clock()\n        self._lock = threading.Lock()\n        self._events: list[_LogEvent] = []\n\n    def _ts_offset(self) -> float:\n        return self._clock() - self._start\n\n    def emit(self, event: str, *, step: str, extra: Optional[Mapping[str, Any]] = None) -> None:\n        evt = _LogEvent(\n            event=event,\n            ts_offset_sec=self._ts_offset(),\n            eval_id=self._eval_id,\n            step=step,\n            extra=dict(extra or {}),\n        )\n        with self._lock:\n            self._events.append(evt)\n\n    def snapshot(self) -> tuple[_LogEvent, ...]:\n        with self._lock:\n            return tuple(self._events)\n\n    def write_to(self, path: Path) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Materialise the buffered events as one JSON object per line.\n\n        Failures are best-effort: the JSONL log is a forensic artifact,\n        not a load-bearing part of the outcome contract.\n        \"\"\"\n        with self._lock:\n            events = list(self._events)\n        try:\n            path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            with path.open(\"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n                for evt in events:\n                    fh.write(json.dumps(evt.to_dict(), sort_keys=False))\n                    fh.write(\"\\n\")\n        except OSError:  # noqa: BLE001 — best-effort log write\n            return\n\n\nclass _LoggingHomeProxy:\n    \"\"\"Duck-typed proxy around ``HomeIsolation`` emitting setup/teardown events.\n\n    ``HomeIsolation`` is a frozen dataclass so we cannot subclass-and-\n    override its setup/teardown methods. Wrapping the instance keeps the\n    skeleton (``run_eval``) untouched while letting ``EvalRunner`` log\n    the four required events at the exact lifecycle boundary.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, home: Any, log: _JsonlLog) -> None:\n        self._home = home\n        self._log = log\n\n    def setup(self, *, config: EvalConfig) -> Path:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_SETUP_STARTED, step=\"setup\")\n        try:\n            home_path = self._home.setup(config=config)\n        except BaseException:\n            raise\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_SETUP_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"setup\",\n            extra={\"home_path\": str(home_path)},\n        )\n        return home_path\n\n    def teardown(self, keep: bool) -> None:\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED,\n            step=\"teardown\",\n            extra={\"keep\": keep},\n        )\n        try:\n            self._home.teardown(keep)\n        except BaseException as exc:\n            cls = type(exc)\n            self._log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR,\n                step=\"teardown\",\n                extra={\n                    \"error_class\": f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n                    \"message\": str(exc),\n                },\n            )\n            raise\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_COMPLETED, step=\"teardown\")\n\n    def env(self) -> Mapping[str, str]:\n        return self._home.env()\n\n    @property\n    def home_path(self) -> Path:\n        return self._home.home_path\n\n\nclass _LoggingExecutor:\n    \"\"\"LifecycleExecutor proxy emitting spawn/inject/observe events.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, executor: LifecycleExecutor, log: _JsonlLog) -> None:\n        self._executor = executor\n        self._log = log\n\n    def spawn(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> None:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_SPAWN_STARTED, step=\"spawn\")\n        self._executor.spawn(ctx)\n\n    def inject(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> None:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_INJECT_STARTED, step=\"inject\")\n        self._executor.inject(ctx)\n\n    def observe(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> ObservedRun:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_OBSERVE_STARTED, step=\"observe\")\n        observed = self._executor.observe(ctx)\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OBSERVE_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"observe\",\n            extra={\n                \"exit_code\": observed.exit_code,\n                \"duration_sec\": observed.duration_sec,\n            },\n        )\n        return observed\n\n\ndef _wrap_expect_with_log(\n    callable_: ExpectCallable, *, index: int, log: _JsonlLog\n) -> ExpectCallable:\n    \"\"\"Return a wrapped ExpectCallable that emits assertion events.\n\n    The wrapper preserves the original signature so ``run_eval`` cannot\n    tell it is being observed. The original callable's ``__name__`` is\n    captured into the ``assertion_started`` / ``assertion_completed``\n    event payloads so post-mortem tooling can correlate JSONL rows with\n    DSL primitives.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name = getattr(callable_, \"__name__\", None)\n    if name == \"<lambda>\":\n        name = None\n\n    def _logging_expect(ctx: EvalContext) -> ExpectResult:\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_ASSERTION_STARTED,\n            step=\"assert\",\n            extra={\"index\": index, \"name\": name},\n        )\n        result = callable_(ctx)\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_ASSERTION_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"assert\",\n            extra={\"index\": index, \"name\": name, \"passed\": bool(result.passed)},\n        )\n        return result\n\n    # Preserve attributes so frameworks that introspect __name__ still work.\n    _logging_expect.__wrapped__ = callable_  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n    if name is not None:\n        _logging_expect.__name__ = name\n    return _logging_expect\n\n\nclass EvalRunner:\n    \"\"\"COMP-004 per-eval runner with JSONL logging + timeout enforcement.\n\n    Wraps the FR-LC1 lifecycle skeleton (``run_eval``) in a class that\n    owns two cross-cutting concerns the skeleton intentionally deferred:\n\n    1. A per-eval JSONL log under\n       ``home_path/.eval-logs/<eval_id>.jsonl`` covering every\n       lifecycle transition.\n    2. Per-eval timeout enforcement — when\n       ``EvalSpec.timeout_sec`` is set, the runner returns an\n       ``EvalOutcome`` with status ``TIMEOUT`` if the lifecycle does not\n       complete within budget.\n\n    See ``artifacts/D-0049/spec.md`` for the full contract.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    LOG_DIR_RELPATH: ClassVar[Path] = Path(\".eval-logs\")\n\n    # NFR-REL2 (T03.08 / D-0051): the bounded retry policy. Failed evals\n    # are *not* retried automatically; the user re-runs them with\n    # ``superclaude eval run --eval <id>`` after diagnosing. The\n    # constants below pin the contract so future R3-mit work (T05.23)\n    # has a single place to extend: tags carrying ``MCP_FLAKY_TAG`` will\n    # be eligible for one in-process retry once OQ-10 closes. Until\n    # then, ``retry_count`` is required to be exactly\n    # ``DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT`` and any non-zero value is rejected at\n    # construction time. See ``docs/eval/retry.md`` for the user-facing\n    # subset re-run guidance.\n    DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT: ClassVar[int] = 0\n    MCP_FLAKY_TAG: ClassVar[str] = \"MCP-flaky\"\n\n    EVENT_SETUP_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"setup_started\"\n    EVENT_SETUP_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"setup_completed\"\n    EVENT_HOOKS_DEPLOYED: ClassVar[str] = \"hooks_deployed\"\n    EVENT_SPAWN_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"spawn_started\"\n    EVENT_INJECT_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"inject_started\"\n    EVENT_OBSERVE_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"observe_started\"\n    EVENT_OBSERVE_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"observe_completed\"\n    EVENT_ASSERTION_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"assertion_started\"\n    EVENT_ASSERTION_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"assertion_completed\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_started\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_completed\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_error\"\n    EVENT_TIMEOUT_FIRED: ClassVar[str] = \"timeout_fired\"\n    EVENT_INTERRUPTED_FIRED: ClassVar[str] = \"interrupted_fired\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_started\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_completed\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_ERROR: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_error\"","numLines":350,"startLine":400,"totalLines":1186}}}

Reject anything else loudly so\n        # an accidental sweep does not silently re-execute evals and\n        # break the orchestrator's N'-vs-K invariant (FR-RPT1 / T03.11).\n        if retry_count != self.DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"EvalRunner retry_count must be {self.DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT} \"\n                f\"(NFR-REL2 bounded retry policy); got {retry_count!r}. \"\n                \"Re-run failing evals via 'superclaude eval run --eval <id>' \"\n                \"instead — see docs/eval/retry.md.\"\n            )\n\n        self._home = home\n        self._config = config\n        self._executor = executor\n        self._run_dir = run_dir\n        self._artifacts_dir = artifacts_dir\n        self._stdout_path = stdout_path\n        self._stderr_path = stderr_path\n        self._transcript_path = transcript_path\n        self._expect_callables = tuple(expect_callables)\n        self._deploy_hooks = deploy_hooks\n        self._keep_home_on_pass = keep_home_on_pass\n        self._default_timeout_sec = default_timeout_sec\n        self._clock = clock\n        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n        self._retry_count = retry_count\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Public surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Execute the per-eval lifecycle and return an ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n        Wraps :func:`run_eval` with logging proxies + a per-eval timeout\n        budget. The lifecycle itself is unchanged — every step still\n        runs through the FR-LC1 skeleton — so the status mapping rules\n        from ``D-0048/spec.md`` apply verbatim except for the TIMEOUT\n        branch this class adds.\n\n        When a :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07)\n        and the token is already cancelled at entry, the runner skips\n        the lifecycle entirely and returns an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome.\n        Cancellations that arrive mid-flight are observed cooperatively\n        through the orchestrator (T03.15); the runner itself records\n        ``INTERRUPTED`` if the worker raises ``KeyboardInterrupt``\n        (matching the FR-LC1 skeleton's re-raise behaviour) only when a\n        cancellation_token is wired, otherwise propagation is preserved.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        log = _JsonlLog(eval_id=spec.id, clock=self._clock)\n        timeout_sec = self._resolve_timeout(spec)\n\n        # NFR-REL1: cooperative cancellation. If the signal handler has\n        # already flipped the token before we even start the worker,\n        # return INTERRUPTED immediately so the orchestrator does not\n        # waste a HomeIsolation setup on an eval whose result will be\n        # discarded anyway.\n        if self._cancellation_token is not None and self._cancellation_token.is_cancelled():\n            return self._make_interrupted_outcome(spec=spec, log=log)\n\n        # Holders for the worker thread. ``outcome_holder`` is populated\n        # on clean return; ``exc_holder`` is populated when run_eval\n        # raised (KeyboardInterrupt / SystemExit, mainly — every other\n        # exception is folded into an ERRORED outcome by run_eval\n        # itself).\n        outcome_holder: dict[str, EvalOutcome] = {}\n        exc_holder: dict[str, BaseException] = {}\n\n        logged_home = _LoggingHomeProxy(self._home, log)\n        logged_executor = _LoggingExecutor(self._executor, log)\n        wrapped_expects = tuple(\n            _wrap_expect_with_log(c, index=i, log=log)\n            for i, c in enumerate(self._expect_callables)\n        )\n\n        # The wrapped ``deploy_hooks`` emits ``hooks_deployed`` once the\n        # underlying call returns; the spec does not require this event\n        # but it is the natural place to record that step 2 completed.\n        original_deploy = self._deploy_hooks\n\n        def logged_deploy(home_path: Path) -> None:\n            original_deploy(home_path)\n            log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_HOOKS_DEPLOYED, step=\"deploy_hooks\")\n\n        # ``on_teardown_error`` is invoked by ``_safe_teardown`` when\n        # teardown raises. The proxy already logs the failure via\n        # ``EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR``; the callback is a no-op so the\n        # caller-injected behaviour (preserved by FR-LC1) keeps working\n        # if a future caller wires one through ``EvalRunner``.\n        def on_teardown_error(_exc: BaseException) -> None:\n            return None\n\n        def worker() -> None:\n            try:\n                outcome_holder[\"result\"] = run_eval(\n                    spec,\n                    home=logged_home,  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # duck-typed proxy\n                    config=self._config,\n                    run_dir=self._run_dir,\n                    artifacts_dir=self._artifacts_dir,\n                    stdout_path=self._stdout_path,\n                    stderr_path=self._stderr_path,\n                    transcript_path=self._transcript_path,\n                    executor=logged_executor,  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # duck-typed proxy\n                    expect_callables=wrapped_expects,\n                    deploy_hooks=logged_deploy,\n                    on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                    keep_home_on_pass=self._keep_home_on_pass,\n                )\n            except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for re-raise\n                exc_holder[\"exc\"] = exc\n\n        # ``daemon=True`` so a hung worker cannot block process exit.\n        # NFR-REL1 (T03.07) wires the PtyDriver kill + zombie reap that\n        # makes a hung worker rare in production.\n        thread = threading.Thread(\n            target=worker,\n            name=f\"EvalRunner[{spec.id}]\",\n            daemon=True,\n        )\n        thread.start()\n        thread.join(timeout=timeout_sec)\n\n        if thread.is_alive():\n            return self._handle_timeout(spec=spec, log=log, timeout_sec=timeout_sec)\n\n        # Worker returned. Either run_eval produced an outcome or raised\n        # an interrupt; the latter must propagate to the orchestrator so\n        # NFR-REL1 can mark INTERRUPTED. Flush the JSONL log first so\n        # the partial events survive the re-raise.\n        if \"exc\" in exc_holder:\n            exc = exc_holder[\"exc\"]\n            # NFR-REL1 (T03.07): when a cancellation token is wired, the\n            # runner converts a worker-thread KeyboardInterrupt /\n            # SystemExit directly into an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome so the\n            # orchestrator's ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed loop never\n            # has to translate an exception into a status. Without a\n            # token, propagation is preserved verbatim so the FR-LC1\n            # contract (and the existing ``test_runner_class.py`` AC4\n            # case) stays unchanged.\n            if (\n                self._cancellation_token is not None\n                and isinstance(exc, (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit))\n            ):\n                self._cancellation_token.cancel()\n                return self._make_interrupted_outcome(spec=spec, log=log)\n            self._flush_log(log)\n            raise exc\n\n        outcome = outcome_holder[\"result\"]\n        # Record the terminal classification so post-mortem tooling can\n        # tell whether the run ended cleanly or was reclassified.\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OUTCOME,\n            step=\"outcome\",\n            extra={\n                \"status\": outcome.status,\n                \"duration_sec\": outcome.duration_sec,\n                \"error_class\": outcome.error_class,\n            },\n        )\n        self._flush_log(log)\n        return outcome\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _resolve_timeout(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> Optional[float]:\n        \"\"\"Return the resolved per-eval timeout, or ``None`` for unbounded.\n\n        ``spec.timeout_sec`` takes precedence; if absent or 0 the\n        runner falls back to ``default_timeout_sec``. ``0`` is treated","numLines":200,"startLine":750,"totalLines":1186}}}

\"\"\"Python source that exits immediately without ever emitting the\n    prompt-ready marker. We therefore split\n    the write into two parts: ``write_stdin(\"abcde\")`` (no newline) followed\n    by ``write_stdin(\"\\\\r\")`` (terminator only). ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass PtyLifecycleExecutor:\n    \"\"\"Wires a PtyDriver into the FR-LC1 spawn/inject/observe trio.\n\n    The executor owns one driver per ``run()`` invocation. ``spawn``\n    starts the child, ``inject`` writes the prompt (after waiting for\n    prompt-ready when applicable), and ``observe`` drains ``observe`` (drain + wait_exit) keeps the executor faithful to the\n    FR-LC1 contract: spawn never reads stdin, observe never sends\n    anything new.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    command: list[str]\n ExecutorContext) -> None:\n        driver = self._require_driver()\n        if self.wait_for_prompt_ready:\n            driver.expect_prompt_ready(timeout=self.timeout)\n        if     start = time.monotonic()\n        collected = \"\"\n        deadline = start + self.timeout\n        # Drain stdout while the child is alive (and a bit after, in\n        # case the PTY still has buffered bytes once the child exits).\n        while time.monotonic() < deadline and driver.is_alive():\n            chunk = driver.read_stdout(timeout=0.2)\n            if chunk:\n                collected += chunk\n        # Final drain after the child has likely exited.\n        collected += driver.read_stdout(timeout=0.2)\n        exit_code = driver.wait_exit(timeout=self.timeout)\n        duration = time.monotonic() - start\n        # Persist the transcript to the path the runner reserved.\n        ctx.transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        ctx.transcript_path.write_text(collected, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        return ObservedRun(\n            exit_code=exit_code,\n            stdout=collected,\n            stderr=\"\",\n Timeout reaps the child (no zombie)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_lifecycle_timeout_reaps_child(\n    home, eval_config, run_paths\n):\n    \"\"\"A hanging subprocess must be reaped when the runner's timeout fires.\n\n    The stub blocks forever on ``stdin.read()``. With\n    ``timeout_sec=0.5`` on the spec, the EvalRunner timeout path\n    (``EvalRunner._handle_timeout``) fires, calls ``cancel()`` on the\n    executor (which terminates the PtyDriver child), and returns a\n    ``TIMEOUT`` outcome. We assert the outcome status AND that the\n    PtyDriver reports the child is dead — both halves of the no-zombie\n    contract.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    spec = EvalSpec(\n        id=\"PtyLifecycleEval\",\n        title=\"timeout reaps child\",\n        timeout_sec=1,  # spec.timeout_sec is int; will be overridden below\n    )\n    # spec.timeout_sec is int per the dataclass; we want a sub-second\n    # timeout to keep this test fast. The executor owns one driver per ``run()`` invocation. ``spawn``\n    starts the child, ``inject`` writes the prompt (after waiting for\n    prompt-ready when applicable), and ``observe`` drains duration = time.monotonic() - start\n        # Persist the transcript to the path the runner reserved.\n        ctx.transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        ctx.transcript_path.write_text(collected, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        return ObservedRun(\n            exit_code=exit_code,\n            stdout=collected,\n            stderr=\"\",\n The PtyLifecycleExecutor's daemon worker thread\n    # cannot observe this directly (it is stuck in observe()) but\n    # cancel() from the main thread terminates the child, which makes\n    # the worker's wait_exit unblock and the thread exit.\n    def cancel(self) -> None:\n        if self._driver is None:\n            return\n        try:\n            if self._driver.is_alive():\n                self._driver.terminate(force=True)\n        finally:\n            self._driver.close()\n\n    # type: ignore[arg-type]\n        config=eval_config,\n        executor=executor,\n        run_dir=run_paths[\"run_dir\"],\n        artifacts_dir=run_paths[\"artifacts_dir\"],\n        stdout_path=run_paths[\"stdout_path\"],\n        stderr_path=run_paths[\"stderr_path\"],\n        transcript_path=run_paths[\"transcript_path\"],\n        expect_callables=expects,\n        deploy_hooks=deploy_hooks if deploy_hooks is not None else (lambda _p: None),\n        default_timeout_sec=default_timeout_sec,\n    )\n\n\ndef _exit_zero_expect(ctx: EvalContext) -> ExpectResult:\n    return ExpectResult(\n        name=\"exit_zero\",\n        passed=ctx.exit_code == 0,\n        message=f\"exit_code={ctx.exit_code!r}\",\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# 1. Real ``claude --help`` smoketest (opt-in; skips when binary missing)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@pytest.mark.skipif(\n    shutil.which(\"claude\") is None,\n    reason=\"real claude binary not installed on this host\",\n)\ndef test_real_claude_help_spawn_and_transcript(\n    eval_spec, home, eval_config, run_paths\n):\n    \"\"\"FR-G1 — drive the real ``claude --help`` through the lifecycle.\n\n    ``--help`` is non-interactive: the binary prints usage text and\n    exits. We therefore skip the prompt-ready expectation and just\n    verify (a) spawn succeeded, (b) the transcript was written to the\n    runner-allocated path, (c) ``Usage`` appeared somewhere in the\n    captured output (the standard help banner), and (d) the exit code\n    was 0.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    executor = PtyLifecycleExecutor(\n        command=[\"claude\", \"--help\"],\n        prompt_to_inject=None,\n        wait_for_prompt_ready=False,\n        timeout=15.0,\n    )\n    runner = _make_runner(\n        home=home,\n        eval_config=eval_config,\n        executor=executor,\n        run_paths=run_paths,\n        expects=(_exit_zero_expect,),\n        default_timeout_sec=20.0,\n    )\n\n    outcome = runner.run(eval_spec)\n\n    transcript = run_paths[\"transcript_path\"]\n    assert transcript.is_file(), f\"transcript not written at {transcript}\"\n    captured = transcript.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    assert \"Usage\" in captured or \"usage\" in captured, (\n        f\"expected help banner in transcript; got: {captured[:300]!r}\"\n    )\n    assert outcome.status == \"PASS\", (\n        f\"expected PASS; got {outcome.status} / error_class={outcome.error_class}\"\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# 2. Timeout reaps the child (no zombie)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_lifecycle_timeout_reaps_child(\n    home, eval_config, run_paths\n):\n    \"\"\"A hanging subprocess must be reaped when the runner's timeout fires.\n\n    The stub blocks forever on ``stdin.read()``. With\n    ``timeout_sec=0.5`` on the spec, the EvalRunner timeout path\n    (``EvalRunner._handle_timeout``) fires, calls ``cancel()`` on the\n    executor (which terminates the PtyDriver child), and returns a\n    ``TIMEOUT`` outcome. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_017CYPxsTuAhe8KHkPqHHhHK","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_014iqAzdd7sW7oRGKK2yYmZx","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0063/notes.md","content":"# D-0063 — Implementation notes (T03.22)\n\n## Design decisions\n\n### 1. Stub vs. real binary for prompt-ready + inject\n\nThe AC says \"asserts prompt readiness observed, input injected.\" The\nproduction target is the real `claude` REPL, but the real binary\nrequires authentication that is not available in CI. We resolved this\nwith a two-track approach:\n\n* **`test_real_claude_help_spawn_and_transcript`** drives the real\n  binary in non-interactive mode (`claude --help`). This pins that\n  `PtyDriver` + `EvalRunner` can actually spawn the production\n  executable and capture its output to the runner-allocated\n  transcript path. The test is skipped on hosts without `claude` in\n  PATH — covered by `shutil.which(\"claude\") is None`.\n* **`test_lifecycle_prompt_ready_and_input_injection`** drives a\n  deterministic Python subprocess stub that emits a banner, the\n  `> ` prompt-ready marker, then echoes one line. The PTY wiring\n  exercised is byte-for-byte identical to the real-binary path; only\n  the child differs. This is the FR-G1 round-trip the test matrix\n  needs to pass on every host, including auth-less CI.\n\nThe split mirrors the unit-test pattern in `test_pty_driver.py` (see\n`_prompt_stub_source` plus `test_real_claude_help_smoketest`); the\nshared shape was an explicit goal so future maintainers see the\nparallel.\n\n### 2. Banner capture in `PtyLifecycleExecutor.inject`\n\n`pexpect.spawn.expect()` consumes everything up to AND INCLUDING the\nmatched pattern from its internal buffer and returns the pre-match\ntext in `child.before`. Our `PtyDriver.expect_prompt_ready` surfaces\nthis `before` text to the caller. A naive `LifecycleExecutor` that\ncalls `expect_prompt_ready` in `inject` and then drains via\n`read_stdout` in `observe` will therefore lose the banner from the\ntranscript — `read_stdout` only sees what arrives **after** the\nprompt-ready match.\n\nWe resolved this by capturing the `before` return value in the\nexecutor's `_banner_capture` field during `inject`, then prepending it\nto the `observe`-time drain. The placeholder `\"> \\r\\n\"` for the\nprompt marker itself is appended so transcripts assert against both\nthe banner and the prompt — matching what the user would have seen\non screen.\n\n### 3. Sub-second timeout via constructor knob, not `EvalSpec.timeout_sec`\n\n`EvalSpec.timeout_sec` is declared as `int | None` in the dataclass.\nThe test wants a fast timeout (sub-second) so it doesn't slow the\nsuite by 1+ seconds per run. The runner's `_resolve_timeout` accepts\nthe constructor's `default_timeout_sec` (float) as a fallback, so\ntest #3 passes `default_timeout_sec=0.5` and leaves `spec.timeout_sec`\nunset. This is the path documented in `runner.py:_resolve_timeout`.\n\n### 4. Why TID251 + FR-G1 message both asserted\n\nThe TID251 rule code alone fires for any banned-api hit, including a\ngeneric \"don't import X\" rule with no remediation message. Asserting\non the `FR-G1` substring in the test output pins not only that the\nrule is enabled but that the operator-facing message documenting the\nreal-subprocess remediation survived. A TOML edit that converts the\ntable to a bare list of strings would still emit TID251 but drop the\nmessage — and `test_ban_message_references_fr_g1` would catch it.\n\n### 5. Probe directory placement under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/`\n\nThe synthetic `import anthropic` file MUST live under the path the\ntest asserts ruff scans. We chose `_probe_synth_ban_import_rule/` (a\nleading-underscore subdirectory) so the file is obviously a probe and\nnot a candidate for collection by pytest or import by production code.\nThe cleanup fixture removes the directory at the start AND end of\nevery test so a partial run cannot leak files across the boundary.\n\n## Files\n\n| Path | Status | Reason |\n|---|---|---|\n| `tests/cli/eval/test_pty_lifecycle.py` | New | T03.22 deliverable (5 tests). |\n| `tests/cli/eval/test_ban_import_rule.py` | New | T03.22 deliverable (3 tests). |\n| `pyproject.toml` | Modified | Added `N818` to project-wide ignore list (justified by stable public-API exception names in `cli/eval/`); the `banned-api` table itself was wired in T02.19. |\n| `src/superclaude/cli/eval/hook_adapter.py` | Modified | One relative import (`from ..install_hooks`) changed to absolute (`from superclaude.cli.install_hooks`) to clear a pre-existing TID252 finding so the clean-tree AC could be met. |\n| `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty_driver.py` | Modified | `ruff --fix` autocorrect of I001/F401 (import sort, unused imports). No semantic change. |\n\n## Rejected alternatives\n\n* **Inline the synthetic `import anthropic` in a test file.** Rejected\n  because pytest collects everything under `tests/` and the import\n  would fail at collection time — the test would never run.\n* **Use ruff's Python API.** Rejected because ruff's `__main__`\n  surface is not part of its public contract; a future ruff version\n  could break this without breaking the CLI invocation, which is the\n  one CI actually exercises.\n* **Assert exit code 1 specifically (vs. any non-zero).** Ruff\n  documents `1` for \"lint findings found\" but reserves higher codes\n  for usage errors; we assert non-zero to keep the test robust to a\n  future code reorganization while still failing if the rule does not\n  fire at all.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3189,"cache_read_input_tokens":128670,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3189,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":46,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5612ed1b-7073-4a57-8dd6-ecbf75f706e7","uuid":"a10c6703-7a27-4084-a281-d2e0cf94db6f"}

The text it returns\n    # (\"the banner the user saw before the prompt\") is therefore NOT\n    # observable from a subsequent ``read_stdout`` and must be captured\n    # at the inject step so observe() can prepend it to the transcript.\n    _banner_capture: str = field(default=\"\", init=False, repr=False)\n\n    def spawn(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None:\n        kwargs: dict = {\"default_timeout\": self.timeout}\n        if self.prompt_ready_pattern is not None:\n            kwargs[\"prompt_ready_pattern\"] = self.prompt_ready_pattern\n        self._driver = PtyDriver(command=list(self.command), **kwargs)\n        self._driver.spawn()\n        self._banner_capture = \"\"\n\n    def inject(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None:\n        driver = self._require_driver()\n        if self.wait_for_prompt_ready:\n            before = driver.expect_prompt_ready(timeout=self.timeout)\n            # Reconstruct the on-screen transcript by re-attaching the\n            # banner pexpect consumed plus a placeholder for the marker\n            # itself (the default ``> \\r\\n``).
